**Chapter 6: Progressions and Series**

21. The 5th term of an Arithmetic Progression (AP) is 18. The sum of the first 10 terms is 255. What is the common difference?
    A) 3
    B) 3.5
    C) 4
    D) 4.5
    E) 5

    **Answer: B) 3.5**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Let the first term be `a` and the common difference be `d`.
    2.  The formula for the n-th term is `a_n = a + (n-1)d`.
        The 5th term is 18: `a_5 = a + (5-1)d = a + 4d = 18`. (Equation 1)
    3.  The formula for the sum of n terms is `S_n = n/2 [2a + (n-1)d]`.
        The sum of the first 10 terms is 255: `S_10 = 10/2 [2a + (10-1)d] = 5[2a + 9d] = 255`.
        Dividing by 5: `2a + 9d = 51`. (Equation 2)
    4.  We have a system of two linear equations:
        (1) `a + 4d = 18`
        (2) `2a + 9d = 51`
    5.  From (1), `a = 18 - 4d`. Substitute this into (2):
        `2(18 - 4d) + 9d = 51`.
        `36 - 8d + 9d = 51`.
        `36 + d = 51`.
        `d = 51 - 36 = 15`. Let me re-check. `S_10 = 255` seems right. `255/5 = 51`. Correct.
        Ah, `2(18-4d) = 36-8d`. Correct. `-8d+9d = d`. Correct. `d = 15`.
        If d=15, a = 18-4(15) = 18-60 = -42.
        Let's check with S10. `S10 = 5[2(-42)+9(15)] = 5[-84+135] = 5[51]=255`.
        This is correct. So the common difference is 15. The options must be wrong. Let me re-design.
        Let `d=3`. Then `a=18-4(3)=6`. `S_10 = 5[2(6)+9(3)] = 5[12+27] = 5[39] = 195`. Not 255.
        Let `d=3.5`. Then `a=18-4(3.5)=18-14=4`. `S_10 = 5[2(4)+9(3.5)] = 5[8+31.5] = 5[39.5] = 197.5`. Not 255.
        My arithmetic seems to be correct. The combination of values in the question leads to `d=15`. I will adjust the question to fit an option.
        Let's work backwards from `d=3`. Let `a=10`.
        `a_5 = 10 + 4(3) = 22`. `S_10 = 5[2(10)+9(3)] = 5[20+27] = 5[47] = 235`.
        New Question: 5th term is 22, sum of first 10 is 235. Find d.
        `a+4d=22`, `2a+9d=47`. `2(22-4d)+9d=47`. `44-8d+9d=47`. `d=3`. This works.

    **21. (Corrected Question) The 5th term of an Arithmetic Progression (AP) is 22. The sum of the first 10 terms is 235. What is the common difference?**
    A) 2
    B) 2.5
    C) 3
    D) 3.5
    E) 4

    **Answer: C) 3**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Let the first term be `a` and the common difference be `d`.
    2.  The 5th term is 22: `a + 4d = 22` (Equation 1).
    3.  The sum of the first 10 terms is 235: `S_10 = (10/2)[2a + (10-1)d] = 5(2a + 9d) = 235`.
    4.  Dividing the second equation by 5 gives: `2a + 9d = 47` (Equation 2).
    5.  From Equation 1, express `a` in terms of `d`: `a = 22 - 4d`.
    6.  Substitute this expression for `a` into Equation 2: `2(22 - 4d) + 9d = 47`.
    7.  `44 - 8d + 9d = 47`.
    8.  `44 + d = 47`.
    9.  `d = 3`.

22. A geometric progression (GP) has a first term of 81 and a common ratio of 2/3. Another GP has a first term of 2 and a common ratio of 3. At which term `n` will the values of the terms in both progressions be equal?
    A) 3
    B) 4
    C) 5
    D) 6
    E) They never become equal.

    **Answer: C) 5**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Let the n-th term of the first GP be `a_n` and the second be `b_n`.
    2.  For the first GP: `a=81`, `r=2/3`. The n-th term is `a_n = 81 * (2/3)^(n-1)`.
    3.  For the second GP: `a=2`, `r=3`. The n-th term is `b_n = 2 * 3^(n-1)`.
    4.  We want to find `n` where `a_n = b_n`.
        `81 * (2/3)^(n-1) = 2 * 3^(n-1)`.
    5.  Rearrange the equation to group the powers of 3 and 2.
        `81 / 2 = 3^(n-1) / (2/3)^(n-1)`.
        `81 / 2 = (3 / (2/3))^(n-1)`.
        `81 / 2 = (3 * 3/2)^(n-1)`.
        `81 / 2 = (9/2)^(n-1)`.
    6.  Express 81 as a power of 9/2 if possible. This is not direct. Let's try another way.
        `81 * (2^(n-1) / 3^(n-1)) = 2 * 3^(n-1)`.
        `81 * 2^(n-1) = 2 * 3^(n-1) * 3^(n-1) = 2 * 3^(2(n-1))`.
        `81 * 2^(n-1) = 2 * (3^2)^(n-1) = 2 * 9^(n-1)`.
        `81/2 = 9^(n-1) / 2^(n-1) = (9/2)^(n-1)`. This confirms the previous step.
    7.  Express 81 as 3^4 and 9 as 3^2.
        `3^4 / 2 = ((3^2)/2)^(n-1)`. This is getting complicated. Let's inspect the terms.
        GP1: 81, 54, 36, 24, 16, ...
        GP2: 2, 6, 18, 54, 162, ...
        The terms are not equal. Let me check my algebra again.
        `81 * (2/3)^(n-1) = 2 * 3^(n-1)`.
        `81/2 = 3^(n-1) / (2/3)^(n-1) = 3^(n-1) * (3/2)^(n-1) = (9/2)^(n-1)`. Correct.
        `40.5 = (4.5)^(n-1)`.
        Logarithms would solve this, but this is General Math.
        Let's re-read the setup. Maybe a term is wrong.
        `a_n = 81 * (2/3)^(n-1)`.
        `b_n = 2 * 3^(n-1)`.
        What if `n=5`? `a_5 = 81*(2/3)^4 = 81*(16/81) = 16`.
        `b_5 = 2*3^4 = 2*81 = 162`. Not equal.
        What if the first term of GP2 was `16/81`?
        `a_n = 81 * (2/3)^(n-1)`. `b_n = (16/81) * 3^(n-1)`.
        `81 * (2/3)^(n-1) = (16/81) * 3^(n-1)`.
        `81 * (2^(n-1)/3^(n-1)) = (16/81) * 3^(n-1)`.
        `81 * 81 / 16 = (3^(n-1) * 3^(n-1)) / 2^(n-1) = 9^(n-1) / 2^(n-1) = (9/2)^(n-1)`.
        `(3^4 * 3^4) / 2^4 = (3^8) / 2^4 = ( (3^2)^4 ) / 2^4 = (9^4) / 2^4 = (9/2)^4`.
        So `(9/2)^4 = (9/2)^(n-1)`. `4 = n-1`. `n=5`. This works.

    **22. (Corrected Question) A geometric progression (GP) has a first term of 81 and a common ratio of 2/3. Another GP has a first term of 16/81 and a common ratio of 3. At which term `n` will the values of the terms in both progressions be equal?**
    A) 3
    B) 4
    C) 5
    D) 6
    E) 7

    **Answer: C) 5**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Formula for n-th term of a GP: `a_n = a * r^(n-1)`.
    2.  For the first GP: `Term1_n = 81 * (2/3)^(n-1)`.
    3.  For the second GP: `Term2_n = (16/81) * 3^(n-1)`.
    4.  Set the terms equal to find n: `81 * (2/3)^(n-1) = (16/81) * 3^(n-1)`.
    5.  Group the constants on one side and the terms with `n` on the other:
        `81 / (16/81) = 3^(n-1) / (2/3)^(n-1)`.
        `81 * 81 / 16 = [3 / (2/3)]^(n-1)`.
        `(3^4 * 3^4) / 2^4 = [3 * 3/2]^(n-1)`.
        `3^8 / 2^4 = (9/2)^(n-1)`.
    6.  Express the left side as a power of 9/2:
        `3^8 = (3^2)^4 = 9^4`.
        So, LHS = `9^4 / 2^4 = (9/2)^4`.
    7.  The equation is now `(9/2)^4 = (9/2)^(n-1)`.
    8.  Since the bases are equal, the exponents must be equal: `4 = n - 1`.
    9.  `n = 5`.

23. For a certain geometric series, the sum of the first `n` terms is given by `S_n = 5(2^n - 1)`. What is the 4th term of this series?
    A) 30
    B) 35
    C) 40
    D) 75
    E) 80

    **Answer: C) 40**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The n-th term `a_n` of a series can be found by taking the sum of the first `n` terms (`S_n`) and subtracting the sum of the first `n-1` terms (`S_(n-1)`). `a_n = S_n - S_(n-1)`.
    2.  We want the 4th term, `a_4`. So, `a_4 = S_4 - S_3`.
    3.  Calculate `S_4` using the given formula: `S_4 = 5(2^4 - 1) = 5(16 - 1) = 5(15) = 75`.
    4.  Calculate `S_3` using the given formula: `S_3 = 5(2^3 - 1) = 5(8 - 1) = 5(7) = 35`.
    5.  Calculate `a_4`: `a_4 = S_4 - S_3 = 75 - 35 = 40`.

24. A ball is dropped from a height of 10 meters. On each bounce, it returns to 3/4 of its previous height. What is the total vertical distance traveled by the ball by the time it comes to rest?
    A) 40m
    B) 60m
    C) 70m
    D) 80m
    E) Infinite

    **Answer: C) 70m**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  This problem involves two infinite geometric series. First, the downward distance, and second, the upward distance.
    2.  **Downward distance:** The ball travels down 10m, then down `10*(3/4)`, then down `10*(3/4)^2`, and so on.
        `D_down = 10 + 10(3/4) + 10(3/4)^2 + ...`
        This is a GP with first term `a = 10` and common ratio `r = 3/4`.
        The sum to infinity is `S_∞ = a / (1-r) = 10 / (1 - 3/4) = 10 / (1/4) = 40` meters.
    3.  **Upward distance:** After the first drop, the ball travels up `10*(3/4)`, then up `10*(3/4)^2`, and so on.
        `D_up = 10(3/4) + 10(3/4)^2 + 10(3/4)^3 + ...`
        This is a GP with first term `a = 10*(3/4) = 7.5` and common ratio `r = 3/4`.
        The sum to infinity is `S_∞ = a / (1-r) = 7.5 / (1 - 3/4) = 7.5 / (1/4) = 30` meters.
    4.  **Total distance:** The total distance is the sum of all downward and upward travel.
        `Total = D_down + D_up = 40 + 30 = 70` meters.
    *Alternative Method:* The initial drop is 10m. After that, every bounce consists of an upward and a downward journey of the same distance. The total distance for these bounces is `2 * D_up = 2 * 30 = 60` meters. Total distance = `10 + 60 = 70` meters.

**Chapter 7: Functions and Mappings**

25. If `f(x) = 2x - 1` and `g(x) = x^2 + 3`, find the value of `x` for which `(f ∘ g)(x) = (g ∘ f)(x)`.
    A) -1
    B) 0
    C) 1
    D) -2
    E) 2

    **Answer: A) -1**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Find `(f ∘ g)(x)`:** This means `f(g(x))`.
        `f(g(x)) = f(x^2 + 3) = 2(x^2 + 3) - 1 = 2x^2 + 6 - 1 = 2x^2 + 5`.
    2.  **Find `(g ∘ f)(x)`:** This means `g(f(x))`.
        `g(f(x)) = g(2x - 1) = (2x - 1)^2 + 3 = (4x^2 - 4x + 1) + 3 = 4x^2 - 4x + 4`.
    3.  **Set them equal:** `(f ∘ g)(x) = (g ∘ f)(x)`.
        `2x^2 + 5 = 4x^2 - 4x + 4`.
    4.  **Solve the resulting quadratic equation:** Rearrange all terms to one side.
        `0 = 4x^2 - 2x^2 - 4x + 4 - 5`.
        `0 = 2x^2 - 4x - 1`. This will need the quadratic formula.
        Let me re-check my work. `(2x-1)^2+3 = 4x^2-4x+1+3=4x^2-4x+4`. Correct.
        `2(x^2+3)-1 = 2x^2+6-1=2x^2+5`. Correct.
        `2x^2-4x-1=0`. `x = (4 ± sqrt(16 - 4(2)(-1)))/4 = (4 ± sqrt(24))/4`. Not a clean answer.
        Let's adjust `g(x) = x^2 - 3`.
        `f(g(x)) = 2(x^2-3)-1 = 2x^2-7`.
        `g(f(x)) = (2x-1)^2-3 = 4x^2-4x+1-3 = 4x^2-4x-2`.
        `2x^2-7 = 4x^2-4x-2` -> `2x^2-4x+5=0`. Discriminant is `16-4(2)(5) < 0`. No real solution.
        Let's adjust `f(x) = x+1` and `g(x)=2x^2-3`.
        `f(g(x)) = (2x^2-3)+1 = 2x^2-2`.
        `g(f(x)) = 2(x+1)^2-3 = 2(x^2+2x+1)-3 = 2x^2+4x-1`.
        `2x^2-2 = 2x^2+4x-1` -> `-2=4x-1` -> `-1=4x` -> `x=-1/4`.
        Let's try to make it work for `x=-1`. So `2(-1)^2-4(-1)-1 = 2+4-1=5 != 0`.
        Let's have `2x^2+4x+2=0`. -> `2(x+1)^2=0`. -> `x=-1`.
        I need `(f ∘ g)(x) - (g ∘ f)(x) = 2x^2+4x+2`.
        Let `f(x) = 3x^2+4x+2` and `g(x)=x^2`. Then `f(g(x)) = 3(x^2)^2+4(x^2)+2 = 3x^4+4x^2+2`. `g(f(x))=(3x^2+4x+2)^2`. Very complex.
        Let's go back to the original `2x^2 - 4x - 1 = 0` and see if I made a mistake setting it up.
        `f(x)=2x-1`, `g(x)=x^2+3`. `f(g(x)) = 2x^2+5`. `g(f(x)) = 4x^2-4x+4`.
        `2x^2+5 = 4x^2-4x+4` -> `2x^2-4x-1=0`. This is correct.
        The question is likely flawed in its design to give a simple integer answer. I will engineer one.
        I want `x=-1` to be a root. So I want the final equation to be something like `(x+1)(ax+b)=0`. E.g., `x^2+x=0` or `x^2-1=0`.
        Let's aim for `(gof)-(fog) = 2x^2+2x = 2x(x+1)`.
        Let `f(x)=x+c` and `g(x)=ax^2+b`.
        `f(g(x))=ax^2+b+c`.
        `g(f(x))=a(x+c)^2+b = a(x^2+2cx+c^2)+b = ax^2+2acx+ac^2+b`.
        `(gof)-(fog) = (2ac)x + (ac^2-c)`. This is linear.
        Let's try `f(x)=x^2` and `g(x)=x+k`.
        `f(g(x))=(x+k)^2=x^2+2kx+k^2`.
        `g(f(x))=x^2+k`.
        `x^2+2kx+k^2 = x^2+k` -> `2kx = k-k^2`. If `k=1`, `2x=0` -> `x=0`. If `k=2`, `4x=2-4=-2` -> `x=-1/2`.
        How about `f(x)=2x+1`, `g(x)=x^2+x`.
        `f(g(x)) = 2(x^2+x)+1 = 2x^2+2x+1`.
        `g(f(x)) = (2x+1)^2+(2x+1) = (4x^2+4x+1)+2x+1 = 4x^2+6x+2`.
        `2x^2+2x+1 = 4x^2+6x+2` -> `2x^2+4x+1=0`. `D=16-8=8`. No clean solution.
        Okay, let's use the first attempt but change a number.
        `f(x)=2x-1`, `g(x)=x^2+3`. `f(g(x))=2x^2+5`. `g(f(x))=4x^2-4x+4`.
        Let's change `g(f(x))`. If `g(x)=x^2-2x+4`, then `g(f(x)) = g(2x-1) = (2x-1)^2-2(2x-1)+4 = (4x^2-4x+1)-(4x-2)+4 = 4x^2-8x+7`.
        `2x^2+5 = 4x^2-8x+7` -> `2x^2-8x+2=0` -> `x^2-4x+1=0`. `D=16-4=12`.
        This is a very difficult type of question to design for integer roots. Let's use `x=1` as the target.
        So we want the final equation `2x^2-4x+2=0` -> `2(x-1)^2=0`.
        We need `(gof)-(fog) = 2x^2-4x+2`.
        `f(x)=x^2`, `g(x)=x-1`. `f(g(x))=(x-1)^2=x^2-2x+1`. `g(f(x))=x^2-1`.
        `x^2-2x+1=x^2-1` -> `-2x=-2` -> `x=1`. This works.

    **25. (Corrected Question) If `f(x) = x^2` and `g(x) = x - 1`, find the value(s) of `x` for which `(f ∘ g)(x) = (g ∘ f)(x)`.**
    A) 0
    B) 1
    C) -1
    D) 0 and 1
    E) No solution

    **Answer: B) 1**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Calculate (f ∘ g)(x):** This is `f(g(x))`.
        `f(g(x)) = f(x - 1) = (x - 1)^2 = x^2 - 2x + 1`.
    2.  **Calculate (g ∘ f)(x):** This is `g(f(x))`.
        `g(f(x)) = g(x^2) = x^2 - 1`.
    3.  **Set the two compositions equal:**
        `x^2 - 2x + 1 = x^2 - 1`.
    4.  **Solve for x:**
        The `x^2` terms on both sides cancel out.
        `-2x + 1 = -1`.
        `-2x = -2`.
        `x = 1`.

26. The function `f(x) = (2x+3)/(x-1)`. Find the formula for its inverse, `f⁻¹(x)`.
    A) `(x+3)/(x-2)`
    B) `(x-3)/(x+2)`
    C) `(x-1)/(2x+3)`
    D) `(x+1)/(x-3)`
    E) `2x-3`

    **Answer: A) `(x+3)/(x-2)`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Replace f(x) with y:** `y = (2x+3)/(x-1)`.
    2.  **Swap x and y:** `x = (2y+3)/(y-1)`.
    3.  **Solve for y:** Our goal is to isolate `y`.
        `x(y-1) = 2y+3`.
        `xy - x = 2y + 3`.
    4.  **Group all terms with y on one side:**
        `xy - 2y = x + 3`.
    5.  **Factor out y:**
        `y(x - 2) = x + 3`.
    6.  **Isolate y:**
        `y = (x + 3) / (x - 2)`.
    7.  **Replace y with f⁻¹(x):** `f⁻¹(x) = (x+3)/(x-2)`.

27. What is the domain of the composite function `h(x) = f(g(x))` if `f(x) = √x` and `g(x) = x^2 - 9`?
    A) `x ≥ 3`
    B) `x ≤ -3`
    C) `-3 ≤ x ≤ 3`
    D) `x ≤ -3` or `x ≥ 3`
    E) All real numbers

    **Answer: D) `x ≤ -3` or `x ≥ 3`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The composite function is `h(x) = f(g(x)) = f(x^2 - 9) = √(x^2 - 9)`.
    2.  The domain of a square root function `√u` is all values of `u` such that `u ≥ 0`.
    3.  In this case, the expression inside the square root is `x^2 - 9`. So, for `h(x)` to be defined, we must have `x^2 - 9 ≥ 0`.
    4.  This is a quadratic inequality. First, find the roots of `x^2 - 9 = 0`, which are `x=3` and `x=-3`.
    5.  These roots divide the number line into three intervals: `(-∞, -3]`, `[-3, 3]`, `[3, ∞)`.
    6.  We can test a value from each interval in the inequality `x^2 - 9 ≥ 0`.
        *   Test `x=-4`: `(-4)^2 - 9 = 16 - 9 = 7 ≥ 0`. True.
        *   Test `x=0`: `(0)^2 - 9 = -9 ≥ 0`. False.
        *   Test `x=4`: `(4)^2 - 9 = 16 - 9 = 7 ≥ 0`. True.
    7.  The domain consists of the intervals where the inequality is true. Therefore, the domain is `x ≤ -3` or `x ≥ 3`. In interval notation, `(-∞, -3] ∪ [3, ∞)`.

28. A piecewise function is defined as `f(x) = { 2x+7, if x < -2; x^2-1, if -2 ≤ x < 3; 11-x, if x ≥ 3 }`. What is the value of `f(f(-3))`?
    A) 0
    B) 1
    C) 8
    D) 10
    E) -13

    **Answer: A) 0**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  This is a nested function evaluation. We must first find the inner value, `f(-3)`.
    2.  Since `-3` is less than `-2`, we use the first rule: `f(x) = 2x + 7`.
        `f(-3) = 2(-3) + 7 = -6 + 7 = 1`.
    3.  Now we need to find `f(f(-3))`, which is `f(1)`.
    4.  The input is now `1`. We check which condition `x=1` satisfies. `1` is between `-2` and `3` (`-2 ≤ 1 < 3`), so we use the second rule: `f(x) = x^2 - 1`.
    5.  `f(1) = (1)^2 - 1 = 1 - 1 = 0`.
    6.  Therefore, `f(f(-3)) = 0`.

**Chapter 8: Graphs of Functions**

29. The graph of `y = f(x)` is transformed to `y = -f(2x) + 3`. Which sequence of transformations correctly describes this change?
    A) Horizontal compression by 1/2, reflect across y-axis, shift up by 3.
    B) Horizontal stretch by 2, reflect across x-axis, shift up by 3.
    C) Horizontal compression by 1/2, reflect across x-axis, shift up by 3.
    D) Vertical stretch by 2, reflect across x-axis, shift right by 3.
    E) Horizontal compression by 2, reflect across y-axis, shift up by 3.

    **Answer: C) Horizontal compression by 1/2, reflect across x-axis, shift up by 3.**
    **Explanation:**
    Let's break down the transformations from `f(x)` to `-f(2x) + 3`. It's best to handle horizontal changes first, then vertical.
    1.  `f(x) -> f(2x)`: This is a horizontal transformation. The factor `b=2` causes a horizontal compression by a factor of `1/b = 1/2`.
    2.  `f(2x) -> -f(2x)`: This is a vertical transformation. The negative sign outside the function causes a reflection across the x-axis.
    3.  `-f(2x) -> -f(2x) + 3`: This is another vertical transformation. Adding 3 causes a vertical shift upwards by 3 units.
    Combining these gives: Horizontal compression by 1/2, followed by a reflection across the x-axis, followed by a vertical shift up by 3. This matches option C.

30. A parabola has the equation `y = -2x^2 + 12x - 11`. What are the coordinates of its vertex and its maximum value?
    A) Vertex (3, 7), Max Value 7
    B) Vertex (-3, -65), Max Value -65
    C) Vertex (3, -11), Max Value -11
    D) Vertex (-3, 7), Max Value 7
    E) Vertex (3, 7), Max Value 3

    **Answer: A) Vertex (3, 7), Max Value 7**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The equation is `y = ax^2 + bx + c` with `a=-2, b=12, c=-11`.
    2.  Since `a` is negative, the parabola opens downwards, and its vertex represents a maximum point.
    3.  The x-coordinate of the vertex is given by the formula `h = -b / (2a)`.
        `h = -12 / (2 * -2) = -12 / -4 = 3`.
    4.  The y-coordinate of the vertex, `k`, is found by substituting `x=h=3` back into the parabola's equation.
        `k = -2(3)^2 + 12(3) - 11`.
        `k = -2(9) + 36 - 11`.
        `k = -18 + 36 - 11 = 18 - 11 = 7`.
    5.  The vertex is at `(h, k) = (3, 7)`.
    6.  The maximum value of the function is the y-coordinate of the vertex, which is 7.

31. The graph of an exponential function `f(x) = ab^x` passes through the points `(1, 12)` and `(3, 108)`. What is the value of the y-intercept?
    A) 2
    B) 3
    C) 4
    D) 6
    E) 9

    **Answer: C) 4**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The y-intercept occurs at `x=0`. The value is `f(0) = ab^0 = a*1 = a`. So we need to find the value of `a`.
    2.  Substitute the two given points into the function's equation to create a system of equations.
        *   For `(1, 12)`: `12 = ab^1` => `12 = ab` (Equation 1)
        *   For `(3, 108)`: `108 = ab^3` (Equation 2)
    3.  To solve this system, a good method is to divide Equation 2 by Equation 1.
        `108 / 12 = (ab^3) / (ab)`.
        `9 = b^2`.
    4.  Since the base `b` must be positive, `b = 3`.
    5.  Substitute `b=3` back into the simpler Equation 1:
        `12 = a * 3`.
        `a = 12 / 3 = 4`.
    6.  Since the y-intercept is `a`, the y-intercept is 4.

32. What is the equation of the horizontal asymptote for the transformed function `y = 5 * (2^(x-3)) - 4`?
    A) `y = 5`
    B) `y = -3`
    C) `y = -4`
    D) `y = 0`
    E) `y = 3`

    **Answer: C) `y = -4`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The parent exponential function is `y = 2^x`. Its horizontal asymptote is the x-axis, `y = 0`.
    2.  Transformations affect the asymptote. We are looking at `y = 5 * (2^(x-3)) - 4`.
    3.  A horizontal shift (`x-3`) moves the graph left or right but does not change a horizontal asymptote.
    4.  A vertical stretch (`5 * ...`) stretches the graph vertically away from the x-axis, but the line it approaches (`y=0`) remains the same. `5 * 0 = 0`.
    5.  A vertical shift (`... - 4`) moves the entire graph, including its asymptote, down by 4 units.
    6.  Therefore, the original asymptote `y = 0` is shifted down by 4 units to become `y = -4`.

**Chapter 9: Trigonometry**

33. If `tan(θ) = -3/4` and `cos(θ) > 0`, what is the value of `sin(θ) + cos(θ)`?
    A) `-1/5`
    B) `1/5`
    C) `7/5`
    D) `-7/5`
    E) `1`

    **Answer: B) 1/5**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Determine the Quadrant:** We are given `tan(θ)` is negative and `cos(θ)` is positive. According to the ASTC rule (All, Sine, Tangent, Cosine), this can only happen in Quadrant IV.
    2.  **Construct a Reference Triangle:** In Quadrant IV, `x` is positive and `y` is negative. `tan(θ) = y/x = -3/4`. We can choose `y = -3` and `x = 4`.
    3.  **Find the Hypotenuse (r):** Using Pythagoras' theorem, `r = √(x^2 + y^2) = √(4^2 + (-3)^2) = √(16 + 9) = √25 = 5`. The radius/hypotenuse `r` is always positive.
    4.  **Find sin(θ) and cos(θ):**
        `sin(θ) = y/r = -3/5`.
        `cos(θ) = x/r = 4/5`. (This confirms `cos(θ) > 0`).
    5.  **Calculate the sum:**
        `sin(θ) + cos(θ) = (-3/5) + (4/5) = 1/5`.

34. Simplify the expression `(sin(2x)) / (1 + cos(2x))`.
    A) `tan(x)`
    B) `cot(x)`
    C) `sin(x)`
    D) `cos(x)`
    E) `sec(x)`

    **Answer: A) `tan(x)`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  This requires the use of double-angle identities.
    2.  The identity for the numerator is `sin(2x) = 2sin(x)cos(x)`.
    3.  The identity for the denominator involves `cos(2x)`. We have three choices for `cos(2x)`. We want to choose the one that simplifies with the `1`. The best choice is `cos(2x) = 2cos^2(x) - 1`, because the `-1` will cancel the `+1`.
    4.  Substitute the identities into the expression:
        `[ 2sin(x)cos(x) ] / [ 1 + (2cos^2(x) - 1) ]`.
    5.  Simplify the denominator:
        `[ 2sin(x)cos(x) ] / [ 2cos^2(x) ]`.
    6.  Cancel common factors. The `2` cancels out, and one `cos(x)` cancels out (assuming `cos(x) ≠ 0`).
        `sin(x) / cos(x)`.
    7.  This is the definition of `tan(x)`.

35. Find the general solution for the trigonometric equation `2cos^2(θ) + sin(θ) = 1`.
    A) `nπ + (-1)^n (π/6)`
    B) `2nπ ± π/3`
    C) `nπ + π/2` and `2nπ - π/6`
    D) `2nπ + π/2` and `nπ + (-1)^n (7π/6)`
    E) `2nπ + π/2`, `2nπ + 7π/6`, `2nπ + 11π/6`

    **Answer: E) `2nπ + π/2`, `2nπ + 7π/6`, `2nπ + 11π/6` (or equivalent)**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The equation has both `cos^2` and `sin`. Use the Pythagorean identity `cos^2(θ) = 1 - sin^2(θ)` to get everything in terms of `sin(θ)`.
        `2(1 - sin^2(θ)) + sin(θ) = 1`.
    2.  `2 - 2sin^2(θ) + sin(θ) = 1`.
    3.  Rearrange into a standard quadratic form:
        `0 = 2sin^2(θ) - sin(θ) - 1`.
    4.  Let `y = sin(θ)`. The equation is `2y^2 - y - 1 = 0`.
    5.  Factor the quadratic: `(2y + 1)(y - 1) = 0`.
    6.  This gives two possible solutions for `y`: `y = -1/2` or `y = 1`.
    7.  **Case 1: `sin(θ) = 1`**
        This occurs when `θ = π/2`. The general solution is `θ = 2nπ + π/2`.
    8.  **Case 2: `sin(θ) = -1/2`**
        The reference angle for `sin(α) = 1/2` is `α = π/6`.
        Sine is negative in Quadrants III and IV.
        Quadrant III solution: `θ = π + π/6 = 7π/6`.
        Quadrant IV solution: `θ = 2π - π/6 = 11π/6`.
        General solutions are `θ = 2nπ + 7π/6` and `θ = 2nπ + 11π/6`.
    9.  Combining all general solutions gives the set in option E. (Note: `nπ + (-1)^n (α)` is the compact general solution for sine, but option E lists the solutions explicitly by period `2π`).

36. The area of a triangle is `15√3 cm^2`. Two of its sides are 10 cm and 6 cm. What is the measure of the obtuse angle between these two sides?
    A) 120°
    B) 135°
    C) 150°
    D) 100°
    E) Cannot be obtuse

    **Answer: A) 120°**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The formula for the area of a triangle given two sides and the included angle is `Area = (1/2)ab sin(C)`.
    2.  We are given `Area = 15√3`, `a = 10`, `b = 6`. We need to find angle `C`.
    3.  `15√3 = (1/2) * 10 * 6 * sin(C)`.
    4.  `15√3 = 30 * sin(C)`.
    5.  `sin(C) = (15√3) / 30 = √3 / 2`.
    6.  The sine function is positive in Quadrants I and II.
    7.  **Quadrant I solution:** `C = sin⁻¹(√3/2) = 60°`. This is an acute angle.
    8.  **Quadrant II solution:** `C = 180° - 60° = 120°`. This is an obtuse angle.
    9.  Since the question specifically asks for the obtuse angle, the answer is 120°.

**Chapter 10: Applications of Trigonometry**

37. From the top of a cliff 100m high, the angle of depression of a boat is 30°. The boat sails directly away from the cliff until the angle of depression is 15°. How far did the boat sail? (Give your answer to the nearest meter).
    A) 173m
    B) 200m
    C) 273m
    D) 373m
    E) 193m

    **Answer: E) 193m** (recalculating: 373 - 173 = 200. No, `100(cot15 - cot30)` -> `100(3.732 - 1.732) = 100(2) = 200`. Let's use `tan`. `100/tan15 - 100/tan30 = 373.2 - 173.2 = 200`. Where does 193 come from? Let's check my values. `tan15 = 0.2679`, `tan30 = 0.5774`. `100/0.2679 = 373.2`. `100/0.5774 = 173.2`. `373.2 - 173.2 = 200`. The answer should be 200. Let's adjust the question.
    Let the second angle be 20 degrees. `tan20 = 0.3640`. `100/0.3640 = 274.7`. `274.7 - 173.2 = 101.5`.
    What if the height was 150m? `150(cot15-cot30) = 150*2=300`.
    Let's stick with the original numbers. There might be a subtler interpretation.
    The answer `E) 193m` suggests `100(cot(15) - cot(30))` is not what's happening.
    Let me check `tan(15)` with sum/diff formula: `tan(45-30) = (1-tan30)/(1+tan30) = (1 - 1/√3)/(1+1/√3) = (√3-1)/(√3+1) = (√3-1)^2 / 2 = (3-2√3+1)/2 = 2-√3 ≈ 0.268`.
    Distance1 = `100 / tan30 = 100 / (1/√3) = 100√3 ≈ 173.2m`.
    Distance2 = `100 / tan15 = 100 / (2-√3) = 100(2+√3) = 200+100√3 ≈ 373.2m`.
    Distance sailed = `D2 - D1 = (200+100√3) - 100√3 = 200m`.
    The answer E is incorrect. I will write the question with the correct answer.

    **37. (Corrected Question) From the top of a cliff 100m high, the angle of depression of a boat is 30°. The boat then sails directly away from the cliff until the angle of depression becomes 15°. How far did the boat sail?**
    A) `100(√3 - 1)m`
    B) `100m`
    C) `100√3 m`
    D) `200m`
    E) `200√3 m`

    **Answer: D) 200m**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Draw a diagram. Let the top of the cliff be T, the base be B, the initial position of the boat be P1, and the final position be P2. The height TB = 100m.
    2.  The angle of depression to P1 is 30°, so the angle of elevation from P1 to T is `∠TP1B = 30°`.
    3.  The angle of depression to P2 is 15°, so the angle of elevation from P2 to T is `∠TP2B = 15°`.
    4.  We have two right-angled triangles, ΔTP1B and ΔTP2B.
    5.  In ΔTP1B, `tan(30°) = TB / BP1 = 100 / BP1`.
        `BP1 = 100 / tan(30°) = 100 / (1/√3) = 100√3` meters.
    6.  In ΔTP2B, `tan(15°) = TB / BP2 = 100 / BP2`.
        `BP2 = 100 / tan(15°)`. Using the identity `tan(15°) = tan(45-30) = 2 - √3`.
        `BP2 = 100 / (2 - √3) = 100(2 + √3) = 200 + 100√3` meters.
    7.  The distance the boat sailed is `P1P2 = BP2 - BP1`.
        `P1P2 = (200 + 100√3) - (100√3) = 200` meters.

38. A ship sails from port A on a bearing of 060° for 10 km to point B. It then changes course and sails on a bearing of 150° for 20 km to point C. What is the distance from port A to point C?
    A) `10√3 km`
    B) `10√5 km`
    C) `10√7 km`
    D) `30 km`
    E) `25 km`

    **Answer: C) `10√7 km`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Draw a diagram. Start at A, draw a North line. The bearing 060° gives path AB = 10.
    2.  At B, draw another North line (parallel to the first). The bearing 150° gives path BC = 20.
    3.  We need to find the length of AC. This forms triangle ABC. We need the angle `∠ABC`.
    4.  Extend the line AB past B. The angle between the North line at B and the line AB is 60°.
    5.  The angle between the South line from B and the line AB is also 60° (alternate interior angles with North line at A).
    6.  The bearing of BC is 150°. This is the angle from the North line at B, clockwise, to BC.
    7.  The angle inside the triangle, `∠ABC`, is the angle between line BA extended and line BC. No, it's the angle between AB and BC.
    8.  The angle from the North line at B to the South line is 180°. The angle from the South line at B clockwise to the line BC is `150° - an internal angle`. No.
    9.  Let's use alternate angles. The angle between the South line at B and the North line at B is 180. The angle from the North line at A to AB is 60. The angle between AB and the south line at A is 120. Using parallel lines, the angle from BA extended past B and the North line at B is 60.
    10. Let's find the angle `∠ABC`. The angle from the North line at B to the line BA extended backwards is 60°. So the angle from the North line at B to AB direction is `60+180=240`. No.
    11. Let's find the angle at B inside the triangle. Draw the North line at B. The angle from this North line to BC is 150°. The angle from the line AB to the North line at B is found using parallel lines. The "back-bearing" of AB is `60+180=240`. The angle from North at B to BA is 240. The angle `∠ABC` is `240 - 150 = 90`. Is that right?
    12. Let's use another method. Angle between North at B and line AB is 60. Angle between AB and East is 30. The angle between North at B and BA is `60+180=240`. The angle from North at B to BC is 150. No, `∠ABC` is not `240-150`.
    13. Look at the North line at B. The line extending South from B is parallel to the North line at A. The angle between the South line at B and AB is 60 (alternate angles). The bearing of BC is 150, so the angle between the North line at B and BC is 150. The angle between the South line at B and BC is `180 - 150 = 30`.
    14. Therefore, `∠ABC = 60° + 30° = 90°`.
    15. Triangle ABC is a right-angled triangle. We can use Pythagoras' theorem to find AC.
        `AC^2 = AB^2 + BC^2 = 10^2 + 20^2 = 100 + 400 = 500`.
        `AC = √500 = √(100 * 5) = 10√5 km`. This is option B.
    16. Let me re-verify `∠ABC`. Angle from North at B to South is 180. Bearing of AB is 060. The line South from B makes an angle of `180-60=120` with AB. This angle is outside the triangle. The interior angle is 60. Yes, alternate interior angles between AB and the South line from B is 60. Yes.
        Bearing of BC is 150. Angle between North at B and BC is 150. Angle between South at B and BC is `180-150=30`.
        `∠ABC` is the angle formed by the two paths = `60 + 30 = 90`. This seems correct. Why is the answer `10√7`?
        That would mean `AC^2 = 700`. This happens if `cos(∠ABC)` is involved.
        `700 = 100 + 400 - 2(10)(20)cos(∠ABC)`.
        `200 = -400 cos(∠ABC)`. `cos(∠ABC) = -1/2`.
        This means `∠ABC = 120°`.
    17. Let's re-calculate the angle `∠ABC`. North line at B. Bearing to B from A is 060. Bearing of C from B is 150. Let's draw it carefully. The angle between North and AB is 60. The angle `∠ABC` is the angle between the line BA and BC. Back bearing of A from B is `60+180=240`. The angle `∠ABC` is clockwise `360-240+150` No. It's the smaller angle. `240-150 = 90`. I keep getting 90.
    18. Let's try again. At B, draw N-S and E-W lines. The line AB makes a 60 deg angle with North. The line BC makes a 150 deg angle with North. The angle between the two lines is `150 - 60 = 90`. Yes. `∠ABC=90`. The answer should be `10√5`.
    19. Perhaps the bearing is from a different line. No, "bearing of" is standard.
    20. Okay, I will trust my geometry and correct the answer.

    **38. (Corrected Question) A ship sails from port A on a bearing of 060° for 10 km to point B. It then changes course and sails on a bearing of 150° for 20 km to point C. What is the distance from port A to point C?**
    A) `30 km`
    B) `10√5 km`
    C) `10√7 km`
    D) `20 km`
    E) `10√3 km`

    **Answer: B) `10√5 km`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Draw a diagram. At point A, draw a North line. The path AB is 10 km at an angle of 60° clockwise from North.
    2.  At point B, draw another North line parallel to the first. The path BC is 20 km at an angle of 150° clockwise from this North line.
    3.  We need to find the length of the side AC of the triangle ABC. To do this, we need the angle `∠ABC`.
    4.  The angle between the North line at B and the line BC is 150°.
    5.  The line segment AB makes an angle of 60° with the North line at A. Because the North lines are parallel, the angle between the line segment AB and the North line at B is also 60° (looking "upwards").
    6.  The angle `∠ABC` is the angle between the line segment AB and BC at point B. This is simply the difference between their bearings measured from the same North line: `∠ABC = 150° - 60° = 90°`.
    7.  Since `∠ABC = 90°`, the triangle ABC is a right-angled triangle with the right angle at B.
    8.  We can use the Pythagorean theorem to find the hypotenuse AC:
        `AC^2 = AB^2 + BC^2`
        `AC^2 = 10^2 + 20^2 = 100 + 400 = 500`.
        `AC = √500 = √(100 * 5) = 10√5` km.

39. The graph of `y = A cos(Bx) + D` represents the tides in a harbor. The maximum height is 14m, the minimum height is 2m, and the time between two successive high tides is 12 hours. Which are the correct values for A, B, and D?
    A) A = 12, B = π/6, D = 8
    B) A = 6, B = π/12, D = 8
    C) A = 6, B = π/6, D = 8
    D) A = 12, B = π/12, D = 7
    E) A = 6, B = π/6, D = 7

    **Answer: C) A = 6, B = π/6, D = 8**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Amplitude (A):** The amplitude is half the difference between the maximum and minimum values.
        `A = (Max - Min) / 2 = (14 - 2) / 2 = 12 / 2 = 6`.
    2.  **Vertical Shift (D) / Midline:** The midline is the average of the maximum and minimum values.
        `D = (Max + Min) / 2 = (14 + 2) / 2 = 16 / 2 = 8`.
    3.  **Period and B:** The period is the time for one full cycle, which is the time between two successive high tides. So, Period `P = 12` hours.
    4.  The relationship between the period `P` and the parameter `B` is `P = 2π / B`.
    5.  `12 = 2π / B`.
    6.  Solve for B: `B = 2π / 12 = π / 6`.
    7.  Therefore, `A = 6`, `B = π/6`, and `D = 8`.

40. What is the period of the function `f(x) = 4 tan(πx/3 - 2)`?
    A) 3
    B) 6
    C) 3/π
    D) π/3
    E) 2/3

    **Answer: A) 3**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The standard period of the tangent function `tan(x)` is `π`.
    2.  For a transformed function `y = A tan(B(x - C)) + D`, the new period is given by `P = (Standard Period) / |B|`.
    3.  In our function `f(x) = 4 tan(πx/3 - 2)`, we can factor out the coefficient of x to see B clearly: `f(x) = 4 tan( (π/3)(x - 6/π) )`.
    4.  The coefficient `B` is `π/3`.
    5.  The new period is `P = π / (π/3)`.
    6.  `P = π * (3/π) = 3`.
    7.  The vertical stretch (4), horizontal shift (-2 or -6/π), and vertical shifts do not affect the period.

**Chapter 11: Coordinate Geometry**

41. Find the equation of the perpendicular bisector of the line segment joining the points A(-2, 5) and B(4, -1).
    A) `y = x + 1`
    B) `y = -x + 3`
    C) `y = x - 1`
    D) `y = -x + 1`
    E) `y = 2x`

    **Answer: A) `y = x + 1`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  A perpendicular bisector does two things: it is perpendicular to the segment, and it passes through the midpoint of the segment.
    2.  **Find the midpoint (M):**
        `M = ( (x1+x2)/2, (y1+y2)/2 ) = ( (-2+4)/2, (5-1)/2 ) = ( 2/2, 4/2 ) = (1, 2)`.
    3.  **Find the slope of segment AB (m_AB):**
        `m_AB = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1) = (-1 - 5) / (4 - (-2)) = -6 / 6 = -1`.
    4.  **Find the slope of the perpendicular bisector (m_perp):** The perpendicular slope is the negative reciprocal of `m_AB`.
        `m_perp = -1 / m_AB = -1 / (-1) = 1`.
    5.  **Find the equation of the bisector:** We have the slope `m=1` and a point it passes through `(1, 2)`. Use the point-slope form `y - y1 = m(x - x1)`.
        `y - 2 = 1(x - 1)`.
        `y - 2 = x - 1`.
        `y = x + 1`.

42. Line L1 is given by `2x - ky = 3`. Line L2 passes through the points (1, 5) and (4, -1). If L1 is parallel to L2, what is the value of k?
    A) -2
    B) -1
    C) 1
    D) 2
    E) -1/2

    **Answer: B) -1**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Find the slope of L2 (m2):**
        `m2 = (-1 - 5) / (4 - 1) = -6 / 3 = -2`.
    2.  **Find the slope of L1 (m1):** Rearrange the equation `2x - ky = 3` into slope-intercept form `y = mx + c`.
        `-ky = -2x + 3`.
        `ky = 2x - 3`.
        `y = (2/k)x - 3/k`.
        The slope `m1` is `2/k`.
    3.  **Use the parallel condition:** For L1 to be parallel to L2, their slopes must be equal: `m1 = m2`.
        `2/k = -2`.
    4.  **Solve for k:**
        `2 = -2k`.
        `k = 2 / -2 = -1`.

43. A line segment has one endpoint at A(3, 8) and its midpoint at M(-1, 5). What are the coordinates of the other endpoint, B?
    A) `(-5, 2)`
    B) `(1, 6.5)`
    C) `(2, 13)`
    D) `(-4, -3)`
    E) `(7, 11)`

    **Answer: A) `(-5, 2)`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Let the coordinates of A be `(x1, y1) = (3, 8)` and the unknown coordinates of B be `(x2, y2)`.
    2.  The midpoint formula is `M = ( (x1+x2)/2, (y1+y2)/2 )`.
    3.  We are given `M = (-1, 5)`.
    4.  Set up equations for the x and y coordinates separately.
        *   x-coordinate: `-1 = (3 + x2) / 2`.
        *   y-coordinate: `5 = (8 + y2) / 2`.
    5.  **Solve for x2:**
        `-2 = 3 + x2` => `x2 = -2 - 3 = -5`.
    6.  **Solve for y2:**
        `10 = 8 + y2` => `y2 = 10 - 8 = 2`.
    7.  The coordinates of endpoint B are `(-5, 2)`.

44. What is the area of the triangle formed by the intersection of the line `3x + 4y = 24` with the coordinate axes?
    A) 12
    B) 18
    C) 24
    D) 36
    E) 48

    **Answer: C) 24**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The triangle is formed by the origin (0,0), the x-intercept, and the y-intercept. This forms a right-angled triangle where the intercepts are the lengths of the two legs.
    2.  **Find the y-intercept:** Set `x = 0`.
        `3(0) + 4y = 24` => `4y = 24` => `y = 6`. The y-intercept point is (0, 6). The length of this leg is 6.
    3.  **Find the x-intercept:** Set `y = 0`.
        `3x + 4(0) = 24` => `3x = 24` => `x = 8`. The x-intercept point is (8, 0). The length of this leg is 8.
    4.  **Calculate the area of the triangle:** `Area = (1/2) * base * height`.
        `Area = (1/2) * 8 * 6 = (1/2) * 48 = 24`.

**Chapter 12: Conic Sections**

45. A circle with equation `(x-2)^2 + (y+5)^2 = 49` is tangent to a vertical line `x = k`. What are the possible values of `k`?
    A) 2 and -5
    B) 9 and -5
    C) 7 and -7
    D) 9 and -9
    E) 5 and -9

    **Answer: B) 9 and -5**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Identify the circle's properties:** The equation is in the form `(x-h)^2 + (y-k)^2 = r^2`.
        *   Center `(h, k) = (2, -5)`.
        *   Radius `r = √49 = 7`.
    2.  **Visualize the situation:** The circle is centered at `(2, -5)`. A vertical line `x=k` is tangent to it. This means the line can touch the circle at its rightmost point or its leftmost point.
    3.  **Find the rightmost point:** The x-coordinate is the center's x-coordinate plus the radius: `k = h + r = 2 + 7 = 9`.
    4.  **Find the leftmost point:** The x-coordinate is the center's x-coordinate minus the radius: `k = h - r = 2 - 7 = -5`.
    5.  The two possible values for `k` are 9 and -5.

46. A parabola has its vertex at (1, 3) and its focus at (1, 5). What is the equation of its directrix?
    A) `y = 1`
    B) `y = -1`
    C) `x = 1`
    D) `x = -1`
    E) `y = 3`

    **Answer: A) `y = 1`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Determine the orientation:** The vertex is (1, 3) and the focus is (1, 5). Since the x-coordinates are the same and the focus is above the vertex, the parabola opens upwards.
    2.  **Find p:** `p` is the directed distance from the vertex to the focus.
        `p = (y_focus - y_vertex) = 5 - 3 = 2`.
    3.  **Find the directrix:** The directrix is a horizontal line (`y = ...`) and is the same distance `p` from the vertex, but in the opposite direction from the focus.
    4.  The equation of the directrix is `y = y_vertex - p`.
        `y = 3 - 2 = 1`.

47. An ellipse has the equation `25x^2 + 9y^2 = 225`. What is the distance between its foci?
    A) 4
    B) 5
    C) 6
    D) 8
    E) 10

    **Answer: D) 8**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Convert to standard form:** Divide the entire equation by 225 to make the right side equal to 1.
        `(25x^2)/225 + (9y^2)/225 = 1`.
        `x^2/9 + y^2/25 = 1`.
    2.  **Identify a^2 and b^2:** The standard form is `x^2/b^2 + y^2/a^2 = 1` (since the denominator under `y^2` is larger, the major axis is vertical).
        `a^2 = 25` => `a = 5`.
        `b^2 = 9` => `b = 3`.
    3.  **Find c:** For an ellipse, the relationship between `a`, `b`, and `c` (distance from center to focus) is `a^2 = b^2 + c^2`.
        `25 = 9 + c^2`.
        `c^2 = 25 - 9 = 16`.
        `c = 4`.
    4.  **Find the distance between foci:** The foci are at `(0, c)` and `(0, -c)`. The distance between them is `2c`.
        Distance = `2 * 4 = 8`.

48. What is the equation of the asymptotes for the hyperbola `9x^2 - 4y^2 = 36`?
    A) `y = ±(2/3)x`
    B) `y = ±(3/2)x`
    C) `y = ±(9/4)x`
    D) `y = ±(4/9)x`
    E) `y = ±(6/1)x`

    **Answer: B) `y = ±(3/2)x`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Convert to standard form:** Divide by 36.
        `(9x^2)/36 - (4y^2)/36 = 1`.
        `x^2/4 - y^2/9 = 1`.
    2.  **Identify a^2 and b^2:** This is a horizontal hyperbola (`x^2/a^2 - y^2/b^2 = 1`).
        `a^2 = 4` => `a = 2`.
        `b^2 = 9` => `b = 3`.
    3.  **Find the asymptote equation:** For a horizontal hyperbola, the equations of the asymptotes are `y = ±(b/a)x`.
    4.  Substitute the values of `a` and `b`:
        `y = ±(3/2)x`.

**Chapter 13: Data Collection and Presentation**

49. For a set of grouped data, the mean was calculated to be 35. It was later discovered that a class midpoint of 20 with a frequency of 10 was mistakenly entered as a midpoint of 40. If the total frequency is 100, what is the correct mean?
    A) 33.0
    B) 34.8
    C. 35.2
    D) 37.0
    E) 33.2

    **Answer: A) 33.0**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The formula for the mean of grouped data is `x̄ = (Σfx) / N`.
    2.  **Find the incorrect sum (Σfx_incorrect):** From the incorrect mean, `35 = (Σfx_incorrect) / 100`.
        `Σfx_incorrect = 35 * 100 = 3500`.
    3.  **Find the error contribution:** The incorrect entry was a midpoint of 40 with frequency 10. Its contribution to the sum was `40 * 10 = 400`.
    4.  **Find the correct contribution:** The correct midpoint was 20 with frequency 10. Its contribution should have been `20 * 10 = 200`.
    5.  **Calculate the correct sum (Σfx_correct):**
        `Σfx_correct = Σfx_incorrect - (Incorrect Contribution) + (Correct Contribution)`.
        `Σfx_correct = 3500 - 400 + 200 = 3300`.
    6.  **Calculate the correct mean:**
        `Correct Mean = Σfx_correct / N = 3300 / 100 = 33`.

50. In a pie chart representing the budget of a company, the angle of the sector for "Salaries" is 108°. If the total budget is $1,500,000, what is the combined budget for all other categories?
    A) $450,000
    B) $600,000
    C) $900,000
    D) $1,050,000
    E) $1,150,000

    **Answer: D) $1,050,000**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Method 1: Calculate Salary budget and subtract.**
        The proportion of the budget for salaries is `(Angle of Sector) / 360° = 108 / 360`.
        `108/360 = 1/3 * 324/360`. No. `108/360 = 54/180 = 27/90 = 3/10`.
        Salary Budget = `(3/10) * $1,500,000 = $450,000`.
        Other categories = `Total Budget - Salary Budget = $1,500,000 - $450,000 = $1,050,000`.
    2.  **Method 2: Calculate the angle for other categories.**
        Angle for others = `360° - 108° = 252°`.
        Proportion for others = `252 / 360 = 126/180 = 63/90 = 7/10`.
        Budget for others = `(7/10) * $1,500,000 = 7 * $150,000 = $1,050,000`.

51. The mean of five numbers is 12. The median is 10. The mode is 8, and it is the only mode. The range is 11. What is the largest number in the set?
    A) 15
    B) 16
    C) 17
    D) 18
    E) 19

    **Answer: E) 19**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Let the five numbers in ascending order be `a, b, c, d, e`.
    2.  **Mean is 12:** `(a+b+c+d+e)/5 = 12` => `a+b+c+d+e = 60`.
    3.  **Median is 10:** The middle number `c` is 10. So the set is `a, b, 10, d, e`.
    4.  **Mode is 8:** Since 8 is the only mode, it must appear more than any other number. As the median is 10, the 8s must be to the left. For it to be the mode, there must be at least two 8s. So, `a=8` and `b=8`. The set is `8, 8, 10, d, e`.
    5.  **Range is 11:** Range = `e - a = 11`. Since `a=8`, `e - 8 = 11` => `e = 19`. The largest number is 19.
    6.  **Verify with the mean:** The set is `8, 8, 10, d, 19`. The sum must be 60.
        `8 + 8 + 10 + d + 19 = 60`.
        `45 + d = 60`.
        `d = 15`.
    7.  The complete set is `8, 8, 10, 15, 19`. This satisfies all conditions: Mean=(8+8+10+15+19)/5=60/5=12. Median=10. Mode=8. Range=19-8=11. The largest number is 19.

52. The variance of a set of 10 numbers is 36. If each number in the set is multiplied by 3 and then decreased by 5, what is the new standard deviation?
    A) 18
    B) 13
    C) 103
    D) 54
    E) 108

    **Answer: A) 18**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Original standard deviation (σ):** Variance `σ^2 = 36`. So, the original standard deviation `σ = √36 = 6`.
    2.  **Effect of transformations on standard deviation:**
        *   Adding or subtracting a constant to every number in a set changes the mean but does **not** change the spread (dispersion). So, decreasing by 5 has no effect on the standard deviation.
        *   Multiplying every number by a constant `c` multiplies the standard deviation by the absolute value of that constant, `|c|`.
    3.  **Calculate the new standard deviation (σ_new):**
        We multiply by 3, so `σ_new = |3| * σ = 3 * 6 = 18`.
    4.  The new variance would be `(σ_new)^2 = 18^2 = 324`. The question asks for the new standard deviation.

**Chapter 14: Probability**

53. A bag contains 4 red balls and `x` blue balls. Two balls are drawn from the bag without replacement. If the probability that both balls are blue is 5/14, what is the value of `x`?
    A) 5
    B) 6
    C) 8
    D) 10
    E) 12

    **Answer: B) 6**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Total number of balls in the bag is `4 + x`.
    2.  The probability of the first ball being blue is `P(1st Blue) = x / (4+x)`.
    3.  After drawing one blue ball, there are `x-1` blue balls left and a total of `3+x` balls left.
    4.  The probability of the second ball being blue, given the first was blue, is `P(2nd Blue | 1st Blue) = (x-1) / (3+x)`.
    5.  The probability of both being blue is the product of these probabilities:
        `P(Both Blue) = [x / (4+x)] * [(x-1) / (3+x)] = 5/14`.
    6.  This gives the equation: `x(x-1) / ((x+4)(x+3)) = 5/14`.
        `14(x^2 - x) = 5(x^2 + 7x + 12)`.
        `14x^2 - 14x = 5x^2 + 35x + 60`.
        `9x^2 - 49x - 60 = 0`.
    7.  This quadratic is hard to solve. Let's try testing the options, which is a valid strategy for MCQs.
        *   **Try x=5:** Total balls=9. `P(Both Blue) = (5/9)*(4/8) = 20/72 = 5/18`. Not 5/14.
        *   **Try x=6:** Total balls=10. `P(Both Blue) = (6/10)*(5/9) = 30/90 = 1/3`. Not 5/14.
        Let me re-check my math. `(6/10)*(5/9) = (3/5)*(5/9) = 15/45 = 1/3`.
        Hmm, let's re-read. Maybe my equation is wrong.
        Total is `4+x`. P(1st Blue) = `x/(4+x)`.
        Remaining is `3+x`. P(2nd Blue) = `(x-1)/(3+x)`.
        Let's try x=6 again. Total=10. `P(1st B) = 6/10`. Rem: 4R, 5B. Total 9. `P(2nd B) = 5/9`.
        `P(Both B) = (6/10)*(5/9) = 30/90 = 1/3`. Still not 5/14.
        Let's re-read the quadratic. `9x^2-49x-60=0`.
        `x = (49 ± sqrt(49^2 - 4(9)(-60)))/18 = (49 ± sqrt(2401 + 2160))/18 = (49 ± sqrt(4561))/18`. Doesn't look good.
        Let's check the fraction 5/14. `14 = 2*7`. The denominators are `(x+4)(x+3)`.
        Let's assume there is a typo in the question and the probability is `1/3`. Then `x=6` would be the answer.
        Let's assume the prob is `5/21`. Then `21(x^2-x) = 5(x^2+7x+12)` -> `16x^2-56x-60=0` -> `4x^2-14x-15=0`. No integer solution.
        What if the probability was `1/6`? Then `6(x^2-x) = 1(x^2+7x+12)` -> `5x^2-13x-12=0`. No integer solution.
        Let's go back to `x=6`, P=1/3. What if there are `y` red balls?
        `P = (6/(y+6)) * (5/(y+5)) = 1/3`. `90 = (y+6)(y+5) = y^2+11y+30`. `y^2+11y-60=0`. `(y+15)(y-4)=0`. `y=4`.
        So the original question with `x=6` and `4` red balls gives `P=1/3`, not `5/14`. The question is flawed.
        I will correct the probability to make `x=6` the right answer.

    **53. (Corrected Question) A bag contains 4 red balls and `x` blue balls. Two balls are drawn from the bag without replacement. If the probability that both balls are blue is 1/3, what is the value of `x`?**
    A) 5
    B) 6
    C) 8
    D) 10
    E) 4

    **Answer: B) 6**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The total number of balls in the bag initially is `4 + x`.
    2.  The probability of the first ball being blue is `P(1st Blue) = x / (4 + x)`.
    3.  After drawing one blue ball, `x-1` blue balls and a total of `3 + x` balls remain.
    4.  The probability of the second ball also being blue is `P(2nd Blue | 1st Blue) = (x-1) / (3 + x)`.
    5.  The probability of both events happening is the product:
        `P(Both Blue) = [x / (4+x)] * [(x-1) / (3+x)] = 1/3`.
    6.  We can solve this equation, but it's faster to test the options for this MCQ.
        *   Let's test `x = 6` (Option B).
        *   Total balls = `4 + 6 = 10`.
        *   `P(1st Blue) = 6/10`.
        *   After one blue is drawn, we have 5 blue and 4 red (total 9).
        *   `P(2nd Blue) = 5/9`.
        *   `P(Both Blue) = (6/10) * (5/9) = 30/90 = 1/3`.
        *   This matches the given probability, so `x=6` is the correct answer.

54. How many distinct 5-letter arrangements can be made from the letters of the word "STATISTICS" if the arrangement must start with a T and end with a C?
    A) 336
    B) 168
    C) 84
    D) 5040
    E) 42

    **Answer: C) 84**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The word is STATISTICS (10 letters total: S=3, T=3, A=1, I=2, C=1).
    2.  We need to form a 5-letter arrangement with the structure `T _ _ _ C`.
    3.  We have used one T and one C. The letters remaining are: S, S, S, T, T, A, I, I. (8 letters left).
    4.  We need to choose 3 letters from these 8 remaining letters to fill the 3 middle spots.
    5.  This is a permutation problem with repetitions. We need to consider different cases for the 3 letters we choose.
        *   **Case 1: The 3 letters are all distinct.**
            The distinct letters available are S, T, A, I. We choose 3.
            Combinations: {S,T,A}, {S,T,I}, {S,A,I}, {T,A,I}.
            For each combination of 3 distinct letters (e.g., S,T,A), they can be arranged in `3! = 6` ways.
            Number of ways to choose 3 distinct letters from 4: `4C3 = 4`.
            Arrangements: `4 * 3! = 4 * 6 = 24`.
        *   **Case 2: Two letters are the same, one is different.**
            The repeated letters available are S (3 of them), T (2 of them), I (2 of them).
            - Choose SS: The third letter can be T, A, or I. (3 choices). Arrangements for {S,S,X} is `3!/2! = 3`. Total: `3 * 3 = 9`.
            - Choose TT: The third letter can be S, A, or I. (3 choices). Arrangements for {T,T,X} is `3!/2! = 3`. Total: `3 * 3 = 9`.
            - Choose II: The third letter can be S, T, or A. (3 choices). Arrangements for {I,I,X} is `3!/2! = 3`. Total: `3 * 3 = 9`.
        *   **Case 3: All three letters are the same.**
            Only S is available 3 times. We can choose {S,S,S}.
            Arrangements for {S,S,S} is `3!/3! = 1`.
    6.  This approach is complex. Let's try another way.
        Let's list the available letters for the middle 3 slots: `{S,S,S, T,T, A, I,I}`.
        We are arranging 3 letters chosen from this multiset.
        This is a hard problem. Let's reconsider. The question might be simpler.
        Maybe it means choose 3 from the remaining 8 and arrange them.
        This is a permutation of a multiset problem. There is no simple formula. The case-by-case analysis is the way. Let me re-check my cases.
        Case 1: XYZ (all distinct). Letters are S,T,A,I. `4P3 = 4!/1! = 24`. (This is choosing and arranging).
        Case 2: XXY (one pair).
          - Pair is SS (we have 3 S's). The third letter Y can be T, A, or I (3 choices). For each choice (e.g., SST), we can arrange it in `3!/2! = 3` ways. Total: `3 * 3 = 9`.
          - Pair is TT (we have 2 T's). Y can be S, A, or I (3 choices). Arrangements `3!/2! = 3`. Total: `3 * 3 = 9`.
          - Pair is II (we have 2 I's). Y can be S, T, or A (3 choices). Arrangements `3!/2! = 3`. Total: `3 * 3 = 9`.
        Case 3: XXX (three of a kind).
          - We have SSS available. Arrangement `3!/3! = 1`.
    7.  Total arrangements = `24 (from Case 1) + 9 + 9 + 9 (from Case 2) + 1 (from Case 3) = 24 + 27 + 1 = 52`.
        My answer is not among the options. Let's rethink the question wording.
        "5-letter arrangements... from the letters of the word". This implies we only use the letters available in STATISTICS. My method is correct, so the question or options are flawed.
        Let's try a simpler interpretation. Perhaps it means "choose ANY 3 letters from the remaining 8 and arrange them". This isn't well-defined.
        Let's assume the word was simpler, like "PARALLEL". `P,A,R,A,L,L,E,L`.
        Arrange 4 letters, start with P, end with E. `P _ _ E`.
        Remaining: `A,R,A,L,L,L`. Choose 2.
        - Distinct: RA, RL, AL. `3P2 = 6`. No, `2! = 2`. RA, RL, AL, AR, LR, LA. 6 ways.
        - Pair: AA, LL. 2 ways.
        Total = 8.
        Okay, let's work backwards from option C, 84.
        Where could 84 come from? `7*12 = 84`. `7C2 * 4 = 21*4=84`. `7P2 * 2 = 42*2=84`.
        The remaining letters are `S,S,S, T,T, A, I,I`.
        Let's assume a simpler question was intended. What if all letters were distinct? e.g. "COMPUTER".
        `C _ _ _ R`. Remaining 6 letters. Choose 3 to arrange. `6P3 = 6*5*4 = 120`.
        Let's correct the question to have a clean answer.

    **54. (Corrected Question) How many distinct 5-letter arrangements can be made from the letters of the word "ARRANGE" if the arrangement must start with A and end with E?**
    A) 20
    B) 40
    C) 60
    D) 120
    E) 24

    **Answer: C) 60**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The word is ARRANGE (A=2, R=2, N=1, G=1, E=1).
    2.  The arrangement must be `A _ _ _ E`.
    3.  We have used one A and the E. The remaining letters to choose from are `{A, R, R, N, G}`.
    4.  We need to arrange 3 letters in the middle slots, chosen from the remaining 5 letters `{A, R, R, N, G}`.
    5.  This is a permutation problem. We can think of it as filling 3 slots.
    6.  **Using Permutation Formula for all 5 letters:** Total letters to arrange = 5. Let's call them `{A, R, R, N, G}`. The number of distinct permutations of these 5 letters is `5! / 2! = 120 / 2 = 60`.
    7.  Since we are arranging all 5 remaining letters in 5 slots (the middle 3, plus the A and R we are *not* using), this is the correct approach. Wait, no. We are choosing 3 letters from the 5 and arranging them.
    8.  Let's use the case method again. The letters to choose from are `{A, R, R, N, G}`.
        *   **Case 1: No repeated letters (all distinct).**
            The distinct letters are A, R, N, G. We need to choose 3 and arrange them. `4P3 = 4! / (4-3)! = 24`.
        *   **Case 2: One pair of repeated letters.**
            The only pair available is RR. We must choose RR. The third letter can be A, N, or G (3 choices).
            For each choice (e.g., R, R, A), the number of arrangements is `3! / 2! = 3`.
            Total for this case = `3 * 3 = 9`.
    9.  Total arrangements = `24 + 9 = 33`. Still not in the options.
    10. Let's re-read the corrected question. "from the letters of the word ARRANGE".
        "start with A and end with E".
        The letters for the middle are chosen from `{R,R,A,N,G}`. My case analysis is correct. The answer is 33.
    11. Let's try to make the question work for the answer 60.
        What if the letters were all distinct? "ORANGE". `O _ _ _ E`. Middle letters from {R,A,N,G}. `4P3 = 24`.
        What if we permute all remaining letters `R,R,A,N,G`? `5!/2! = 60`. Why would we do that?
        Maybe the question means "how many 5 letter words can be formed using the letters `A,R,R,N,G`". That is exactly 60.
        So the question must mean "How many distinct permutations are there of the letters `{A,R,R,N,G}`?".
        This is a poorly worded question, but is a common trap.

    **54. (Final Corrected Question) An arrangement is to be made using the letters {A, R, R, N, G}. How many distinct 5-letter arrangements are possible?**
    A) 20
    B) 40
    C) 60
    D) 120
    E) 30

    **Answer: C) 60**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  We are asked to find the number of distinct arrangements of 5 letters where some letters are repeated.
    2.  The set of letters is {A, R, R, N, G}. Total number of letters `n = 5`.
    3.  The letter 'R' is repeated 2 times. The other letters (A, N, G) appear once.
    4.  The formula for permutations with repetitions is `n! / (n1! * n2! * ...)` where `n1, n2, ...` are the frequencies of the repeated letters.
    5.  Number of arrangements = `5! / 2!`.
    6.  `5! = 5 * 4 * 3 * 2 * 1 = 120`.
    7.  `2! = 2 * 1 = 2`.
    8.  Number of arrangements = `120 / 2 = 60`.

55. A committee of 4 people is to be selected from a group of 6 men and 5 women. What is the probability that the committee consists of more women than men?

    **55. (Corrected Question) A committee of 4 people is to be selected from a group of 6 men and 5 women. What is the probability that the committee consists of more women than men?**
    A) 13/66
    B) 1/3
    C) 1/6
    D) 2/11
    E) 15/66

    **Answer: A) 13/66**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  **Total possible committees:** We choose 4 people from a total of `6 + 5 = 11` people. This is a combination problem.
        `n(S) = 11C4 = 11! / (4! * 7!) = (11 * 10 * 9 * 8) / (4 * 3 * 2 * 1) = 330`.
    2.  **Favorable outcomes (more women than men):** For a committee of 4, this means either (3 Women, 1 Man) or (4 Women, 0 Men).
    3.  **Calculate ways for (3 Women, 1 Man):**
        Ways to choose 3 women from 5: `5C3 = 10`.
        Ways to choose 1 man from 6: `6C1 = 6`.
        Total ways for this case = `10 * 6 = 60`.
    4.  **Calculate ways for (4 Women, 0 Men):**
        Ways to choose 4 women from 5: `5C4 = 5`.
        Ways to choose 0 men from 6: `6C0 = 1`.
        Total ways for this case = `5 * 1 = 5`.
    5.  **Total favorable outcomes:** `n(E) = 60 + 5 = 65`.
    6.  **Calculate the probability:**
        `P(E) = n(E) / n(S) = 65 / 330`.
    7.  Simplify the fraction by dividing numerator and denominator by 5: `13 / 66`.

56. A biased coin is tossed 3 times. The probability of getting a head (H) is 2/3. What is the probability of getting at least two heads?
    A) 8/27
    B) 12/27
    C) 16/27
    D) 20/27
    E) 24/27

    **Answer: D) 20/27**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  `P(Head) = p = 2/3`. `P(Tail) = q = 1 - 2/3 = 1/3`. `n=3` trials.
    2.  "At least two heads" means getting exactly 2 heads OR exactly 3 heads.
    3.  **Probability of exactly 2 heads (HHT, HTH, THH):**
        This follows the binomial probability formula `P(X=k) = nCk * p^k * q^(n-k)`.
        `P(X=2) = 3C2 * (2/3)^2 * (1/3)^1 = 3 * (4/9) * (1/3) = 12/27`.
    4.  **Probability of exactly 3 heads (HHH):**
        `P(X=3) = 3C3 * (2/3)^3 * (1/3)^0 = 1 * (8/27) * 1 = 8/27`.
    5.  **Total Probability:** Since these are mutually exclusive events, we add their probabilities.
        `P(at least 2 heads) = P(X=2) + P(X=3) = 12/27 + 8/27 = 20/27`.

**Chapter 15: Inferential Statistics**

57. A researcher wants to study the average salary of software developers in a country. She divides the country into 5 regions (North, South, East, West, Central), randomly selects 2 of these regions, and then surveys every software developer in those 2 selected regions. What sampling technique is this?
    A) Simple Random Sampling
    B) Stratified Sampling
    C) Systematic Sampling
    D) One-Stage Cluster Sampling
    E) Convenience Sampling

    **Answer: D) One-Stage Cluster Sampling**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The population (software developers) is divided into groups, or **clusters** (the 5 regions).
    2.  A random sample of these **clusters** is taken (2 regions are randomly selected).
    3.  **All** members within the selected clusters are then included in the sample (surveying *every* developer in those 2 regions).
    4.  This two-step process of sampling clusters and then sampling all individuals within them is the definition of **one-stage cluster sampling**.
    5.  It is not Stratified Sampling because not all strata (regions) are represented; a random sample of strata is taken. It is not Simple Random Sampling because not every individual developer had an equal chance of being selected (a developer in an unselected region had zero chance).

58. A 95% confidence interval for the mean height of a plant species is found to be (25.4 cm, 28.6 cm). Which statement is the most accurate interpretation of this interval?
    A) There is a 95% probability that the true mean height of the species is between 25.4 cm and 28.6 cm.
    B) 95% of all plants of this species have a height between 25.4 cm and 28.6 cm.
    C) We are 95% confident that a randomly selected plant will have a height between 25.4 cm and 28.6 cm.
    D) If we were to repeat this sampling process many times, we would expect 95% of the calculated confidence intervals to contain the true mean height of the species.
    E) The sample mean is 27.0 cm, and it will be correct 95% of the time.

    **Answer: D)**
    **Explanation:**
    This question tests the subtle but crucial interpretation of a confidence interval.
    *   A) is incorrect. The true mean is a fixed, unknown parameter. It either is or is not in the interval. The 95% probability refers to the process, not a specific interval.
    *   B) is incorrect. A confidence interval is for a population **parameter** (the mean), not for the range of individual data points. The range of individual heights will be much wider.
    *   C) is incorrect. This again confuses an interval for the mean with a prediction interval for a single observation.
    *   D) is the correct, formal interpretation. The "95% confidence" is in the **method** or **procedure**. It means the procedure used to generate the interval will capture the true population parameter 95% of the time in the long run.
    *   E) is incorrect. The sample mean is the center of the interval, `(25.4+28.6)/2 = 27.0 cm`, but the interval is about the uncertainty of this estimate, not about the sample mean being "correct".

59. A study finds a strong positive correlation (r = 0.85) between the number of hours students spend in the library per week and their GPA. Which conclusion is most warranted?
    A) Spending more time in the library causes an increase in GPA.
    B) Increasing a student's GPA will cause them to spend more time in the library.
    C) Students who spend more time in the library tend to have higher GPAs.
    D) Any student who spends no time in the library will have a low GPA.
    E) This correlation is too high to be a coincidence, so it must be causal.

    **Answer: C) Students who spend more time in the library tend to have higher GPAs.**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The key concept is **correlation does not imply causation**.
    2.  A) and B) both state a causal relationship, which cannot be concluded from a correlation alone. There could be a lurking variable (e.g., student motivation, good study habits) that causes both more library time and a higher GPA.
    3.  C) is the most accurate and cautious statement. It simply describes the **association** or **tendency** observed in the data without claiming a cause-and-effect link. This is precisely what correlation allows us to say.
    4.  D) is an overgeneralization. A correlation describes a trend, not a deterministic rule for every individual.
    5.  E) is false. A strong correlation can absolutely be the result of a lurking variable or coincidence. The strength of the correlation does not prove causality.

60. In a hypothesis test, a researcher obtains a p-value of 0.03. Using a significance level of α = 0.05, what is the correct decision and conclusion?
    A) Fail to reject H₀; there is not enough evidence to support H₁.
    B) Reject H₀; there is strong evidence to support H₁.
    C) Reject H₁; there is strong evidence to support H₀.
    D) Fail to reject H₀; there is strong evidence to support H₀.
    E) The result is inconclusive and the experiment must be repeated.

    **Answer: B) Reject H₀; there is strong evidence to support H₁.**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The rule for making a decision in a hypothesis test is: If `p-value ≤ α`, we reject the null hypothesis (H₀). If `p-value > α`, we fail to reject the null hypothesis.
    2.  Here, the `p-value = 0.03` and the significance level `α = 0.05`.
    3.  Since `0.03 ≤ 0.05`, the condition is met to **reject the null hypothesis (H₀)**.
    4.  Rejecting H₀ means we have found a statistically significant result. This provides evidence **in favor of the alternative hypothesis (H₁)**.
    5.  Therefore, the correct course of action is to reject H₀ and conclude that there is strong evidence to support H₁.

**Mixed Comprehensive Questions (61-75)**

61. What is the sum of the first 20 terms of an arithmetic sequence whose 3rd term is 7 and 7th term is 15?
    A) 580
    B) 600
    C) 620
    D) 640
    E) 660

    **Answer: C) 620**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  `a_3 = a + 2d = 7`.
    2.  `a_7 = a + 6d = 15`.
    3.  Subtracting the first equation from the second: `(a+6d) - (a+2d) = 15 - 7` => `4d = 8` => `d = 2`.
    4.  Substitute `d=2` into the first equation: `a + 2(2) = 7` => `a + 4 = 7` => `a = 3`.
    5.  Find the sum of the first 20 terms: `S_20 = n/2 [2a + (n-1)d] = 20/2 [2(3) + (19)2] = 10[6 + 38] = 10[44] = 440`.
    Let me re-check. `a_3 = 3+2(2)=7`. Correct. `a_7 = 3+6(2)=15`. Correct. `S_20 = 10[6+38]=440`.
    The options are wrong. Let's make the answer 620.
    `10[2a+19d]=620` -> `2a+19d=62`.
    `a+2d=7` -> `a=7-2d`.
    `2(7-2d)+19d=62` -> `14-4d+19d=62` -> `15d=48` -> `d=3.2`.
    This is getting messy. Let's adjust the original terms.
    `a_3=8`, `a_7=20`. `4d=12` -> `d=3`. `a+2(3)=8` -> `a=2`.
    `S_20 = 10[2(2)+19(3)] = 10[4+57] = 10[61] = 610`.
    Let's adjust again. `a_3=5`, `a_7=17`. `4d=12`->`d=3`. `a+2(3)=5`->`a=-1`.
    `S_20 = 10[2(-1)+19(3)] = 10[-2+57] = 10[55]=550`.
    What if `d=4`? `a+8=7`->`a=-1`. `a+24=15`->`a=-9`. Doesn't work.
    I will use `a=3, d=2` and correct the options.

    **61. (Corrected Question) What is the sum of the first 20 terms of an arithmetic sequence whose 3rd term is 7 and 7th term is 15?**
    A) 400
    B) 420
    C) 440
    D) 460
    E) 480

    **Answer: C) 440**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Let the first term be `a` and common difference be `d`.
    2.  `a_3 = a + 2d = 7` (Eq. 1).
    3.  `a_7 = a + 6d = 15` (Eq. 2).
    4.  Subtract Eq. 1 from Eq. 2: `(a + 6d) - (a + 2d) = 15 - 7` => `4d = 8` => `d = 2`.
    5.  Substitute `d=2` into Eq. 1: `a + 2(2) = 7` => `a = 3`.
    6.  Find the sum of the first 20 terms using `S_n = n/2 [2a + (n-1)d]`:
        `S_20 = (20/2) [2(3) + (20-1)2] = 10 [6 + 19*2] = 10 [6 + 38] = 10[44] = 440`.

62. The equation of a line is `(x/p) + (y/q) = 1`. This line is perpendicular to `y = 2x + 5` and passes through `(4, 3)`. Find the value of `p+q`.
    A) 15
    B) 10
    C) 5
    D) -5
    E) -10

    **Answer: A) 15**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The slope of the line `y=2x+5` is `m1 = 2`.
    2.  The required line is perpendicular, so its slope `m2` is the negative reciprocal of `m1`: `m2 = -1/2`.
    3.  The line passes through `(4, 3)`. Using point-slope form `y-y1=m(x-x1)`:
        `y - 3 = (-1/2)(x - 4)`.
        `2(y - 3) = -1(x - 4)`.
        `2y - 6 = -x + 4`.
        `x + 2y = 10`.
    4.  We need to convert this to intercept form `x/p + y/q = 1`. To do this, divide by the constant on the right side (10):
        `(x/10) + (2y/10) = 1`.
        `(x/10) + (y/5) = 1`.
    5.  By comparing this to `x/p + y/q = 1`, we see that `p = 10` and `q = 5`.
    6.  The value of `p+q` is `10 + 5 = 15`.

63. Let `f(x) = |x - 3|`. The solution set of the inequality `f(x) < f(x+2)` is:
    A) `x < 2.5`
    B) `x > 2.5`
    C) `x < 0.5`
    D) `x > 0.5`
    E) `x < -0.5`

    **Answer: A) `x < 2.5`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The inequality is `|x - 3| < |x + 2|`.
    2.  A good way to solve inequalities with absolute values on both sides is to square both sides. Since both sides are non-negative, this preserves the inequality.
        `(x - 3)^2 < (x + 2)^2`.
    3.  Expand both sides:
        `x^2 - 6x + 9 < x^2 + 4x + 4`.
    4.  The `x^2` terms cancel out.
        `-6x + 9 < 4x + 4`.
    5.  Solve the resulting linear inequality. Group x terms on one side and constants on the other.
        `9 - 4 < 4x + 6x`.
        `5 < 10x`.
        `5/10 < x`.
        `0.5 < x`.
        Let me re-check.
        `|x-3|` is distance from 3. `|x+2|` is distance from -2.
        We want the point `x` to be closer to 3 than to -2. The midpoint between -2 and 3 is `(-2+3)/2 = 0.5`.
        If x is to the right of 0.5, it is closer to 3. For example, x=1. `|1-3|=2`, `|1+2|=3`. `2<3`. True.
        So the solution should be `x > 0.5`. My algebraic solution was `0.5 < x`.
        Where did I make a mistake? Ah, `|x+2|`. Let me trace my steps. `f(x+2) = |(x+2)-3| = |x-1|`.
        The inequality is `|x-3| < |x-1|`.
        Squaring: `(x-3)^2 < (x-1)^2`.
        `x^2 - 6x + 9 < x^2 - 2x + 1`.
        `-6x + 9 < -2x + 1`.
        `8 < 4x`.
        `2 < x` or `x > 2`. Let's re-read the question. `f(x+2)` not `f(x)+2`.
        Let's try again with `f(x+2) = |(x+2)-3| = |x-1|`. My calculation seems correct. Let me use the midpoint logic.
        Distance from 3 should be less than distance from 1. Midpoint is `(3+1)/2=2`.
        Points to the right of 2 are closer to 3. E.g., x=2.5. `|2.5-3|=0.5`, `|2.5-1|=1.5`. `0.5<1.5`. True.
        So `x > 2`.
        Let me re-read the question again. `f(x)=|x-3|`. `f(x+2)=|(x+2)-3|=|x-1|`.
        `|x-3| < |x-1|`.
        `-6x+9 < -2x+1`.
        `8 < 4x`.
        `x > 2`.
        Okay, the options are wrong. Let's make the function `f(x)=|x|`.
        Then `|x| < |x+2|`. `x^2 < (x+2)^2 = x^2+4x+4`.
        `0 < 4x+4`. `-4 < 4x`. `x > -1`.
        Let's re-read my original calculation.
        `f(x)=|x-3|`, `f(x+2)=|(x+2)-3|=|x-1|`. No, `f(x+2) = |(x+2)-3|` is not right.
        `f(x) = |x-3|`.
        `f(A) = |A-3|`. Let `A = x+2`.
        `f(x+2) = |(x+2)-3| = |x-1|`. Yes, it is right.
        Let's try again with the `f(x)=|x-3|` and `f(x) < f(x+2)` from the prompt... `f(x+2)` is NOT `|x+2|`.
        `f(x) = |x-3|`. Then `f(x+2)` is `|(x+2)-3| = |x-1|`.
        So the inequality is `|x-3| < |x-1|`.
        Square both sides: `x^2 - 6x + 9 < x^2 - 2x + 1`.
        `-6x + 9 < -2x + 1`.
        `8 < 4x`.
        `x > 2`.
        The provided answer `x < 2.5` is wrong.
        Let's assume the question meant `f(x) = |x|`. Then `f(x+2) = |x+2|`.
        `|x| < |x+2|`. Square it: `x^2 < x^2+4x+4`. `0 < 4x+4`. `-4 < 4x`. `-1 < x`.

    **63. (Corrected Question) Given `f(x) = |2x - 1|`. For which values of `x` is `f(x) > 5`?**
    A) `-2 < x < 3`
    B) `x < -2` or `x > 3`
    C) `x < -3` or `x > 2`
    D) `-3 < x < 2`
    E) `x > 3`

    **Answer: B) `x < -2` or `x > 3`**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The inequality is `|2x - 1| > 5`.
    2.  This type of absolute value inequality `|A| > B` splits into two separate inequalities: `A > B` or `A < -B`.
    3.  **Case 1:** `2x - 1 > 5`.
        `2x > 6`.
        `x > 3`.
    4.  **Case 2:** `2x - 1 < -5`.
        `2x < -4`.
        `x < -2`.
    5.  The solution set is the union of the solutions from both cases: `x < -2` or `x > 3`.

64. A set of data has a mean of 50 and a standard deviation of 8. According to the Empirical Rule (for bell-shaped distributions), what percentage of the data would be expected to fall between 34 and 66?
    A) 34%
    B) 68%
    C) 95%
    D) 99.7%
    E) 50%

    **Answer: C) 95%**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  The Empirical Rule states that for a normal (bell-shaped) distribution:
        *   ~68% of data is within 1 standard deviation of the mean (`μ ± σ`).
        *   ~95% of data is within 2 standard deviations of the mean (`μ ± 2σ`).
        *   ~99.7% of data is within 3 standard deviations of the mean (`μ ± 3σ`).
    2.  Given `μ = 50` and `σ = 8`.
    3.  We need to determine how many standard deviations the interval (34, 66) represents.
    4.  Lower bound: `34 = 50 - 16 = 50 - 2*8 = μ - 2σ`.
    5.  Upper bound: `66 = 50 + 16 = 50 + 2*8 = μ + 2σ`.
    6.  The interval (34, 66) corresponds to `μ ± 2σ`.
    7.  According to the Empirical Rule, approximately 95% of the data falls within this range.

65. If `log₂(x) + log₂(x-2) = 3`, what is the value of x?
    A) 4
    B) -2
    C) 4 and -2
    D) 3
    E) 8

    **Answer: A) 4**
    **Explanation:**
    1.  Use the logarithm product rule: `log_b(M) + log_b(N) = log_b(MN)`.
        `log₂(x(x-2)) = 3`.
    2.  Convert the logarithmic equation to its equivalent exponential form: `log_b(A) = C` is equivalent to `b^C = A`.
        `2^3 = x(x-2)`.
    3.  `8 = x^2 - 2x`.
    4.  Rearrange into a standard quadratic equation: `x^2 - 2x - 8 = 0`.
    5.  Factor the quadratic: `(x - 4)(x + 2) = 0`.
    6.  The possible solutions are `x = 4` and `x = -2`.
    7.  **Check for valid domains:** The domain of a logarithm `log_b(A)` requires the argument `A` to be positive.
        *   `log₂(x)` requires `x > 0`.
        *   `log₂(x-2)` requires `x - 2 > 0`, which means `x > 2`.
    8.  The solution `x = -2` is extraneous because it violates both conditions.
    9.  The solution `x = 4` is valid because it satisfies `x > 2`.
    10. Therefore, the only valid solution is `x = 4`.
